  4203	    14	/**
  4204	    15	 * Rename with retry on EPERM (Windows AV/indexer lock pattern): 3 attempts total.
  4205	    16	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} from @param {string} to
  4206	    17	 */
  4207	    18	function renameWithRetry(fs, from, to) {
  4208	    19	  let lastErr;
  4209	    20	  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 3; attempt += 1) {
  4210	    21	    try {
  4211	    22	      fs.renameSync(from, to);
  4212	    23	      return;
  4213	    24	    } catch (err) {
  4214	    25	      lastErr = err;
  4215	    26	      if (!err || /** @type {any} */ (err).code !== 'EPERM') throw err;
  4216	    27	    }
  4217	    28	  }
  4218	    29	  throw lastErr;
  4219	    30	}

  4383	    65	function initRepo(prefix, spec) {
  4384	    66	  // Canonicalize: git resolves worktree paths to their realpath (on macOS the
  4385	    67	  // tmpdir is a /var -> /private/var symlink), so the repo root must be the
  4386	    68	  // realpath too or `git worktree list` paths won't match worktreePaths().

  4393	    75	  // only tracked change would be the .worktrees/ line — pre-seeding removes it).
  4394	    76	  nodeFs.writeFileSync(join(cwd, '.gitignore'), '.handoff/\n.worktrees/\n');

  4402	    84	
  4403	    85	function makeRealIo(cwd, { superviseChild, now = NOW } = {}) {
  4404	    86	  const out = [];
  4405	    87	  const err = [];
  4406	    88	  let tokenN = 0;
  4407	    89	  return {
  4408	    90	    cwd,
  4409	    91	    env: { ...process.env },
  4410	    92	    stdin: '',
  4411	    93	    stdout: { write: (s) => (out.push(String(s)), true) },
  4412	    94	    stderr: { write: (s) => (err.push(String(s)), true) },
  4413	    95	    fs: nodeFs,
  4414	    96	    execFile: (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => pexec(cmd, args, { ...opts, encoding: 'utf8' }),
  4415	    97	    now: () => now,
  4416	    98	    host: 'e2e-host',
  4417	    99	    pid: process.pid,
  4418	   100	    startTime: Math.round(performance.timeOrigin),
  4419	   101	    processAlive: (pid) => { try { process.kill(pid, 0); return true; } catch (e) { return /** @type {any} */ (e).code === 'EPERM'; } },
  4420	   102	    platform: process.platform,
  4421	   103	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${(tokenN += 1)}`,
  4422	   104	    superviseChild,
  4423	   105	    stdoutText: () => out.join(''),
  4424	   106	    stderrText: () => err.join(''),
  4425	   107	  };
  4426	   108	}

  4443	   125	  function realWriterRunner() {
  4444	   126	    let n = 0;
  4445	   127	    return async (childSpec, opts) => {
  4446	   128	      const args = (childSpec.args ?? []).map(String);
  4447	   129	      if (valAfter(args, '-s') === 'workspace-write') {

  4451	   133	        git(seat, ['add', '.']);
  4452	   134	        git(seat, ['commit', '-q', '-m', `subtask change ${n}`], childSpec.env);
  4453	   135	      }
  4454	   136	      return { timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath };
  4455	   137	    };
  4456	   138	  }

  4543	   225	  // A scripted loop runner: returns the next result and writes its log so the
  4544	   226	  // supervisor classifies the transcript from the child's LOG file.
  4545	   227	  function scriptedRunner(script) {
  4546	   228	    const queue = [...script];
  4547	   229	    const calls = [];
  4548	   230	    const fn = async (childSpec, opts) => {
  4549	   231	      calls.push({ command: childSpec.command, args: (childSpec.args ?? []).map(String) });
  4550	   232	      const r = queue.shift() ?? { verdict: 'APPROVED' };
  4551	   233	      if (typeof r.logContent === 'string' && opts.logPath) {
  4552	   234	        nodeFs.mkdirSync(opts.logPath.slice(0, opts.logPath.lastIndexOf('/')), { recursive: true });
  4553	   235	        nodeFs.writeFileSync(opts.logPath, r.logContent);
  4554	   236	      }
  4555	   237	      return { timedOut: false, exitCode: r.exitCode ?? 0, verdict: r.verdict ?? 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath };
  4556	   238	    };
  4557	   239	    fn.calls = calls;
  4558	   240	    return fn;
  4559	   241	  }

+// B8 (G2) — the run-lock acquisition must be ATOMIC, not check-then-write.
+describe('loop run — atomic run-lock acquisition (B8)', () => {
+  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
+  const LOCK = `${DIR}/supervisor.lock`;
+
+  it('RED (B8): a competitor lock that appears in the check→write GAP is NOT clobbered — the run refuses (exit 1)', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    io.processAlive = (p) => p === 55555; // the competitor supervisor is LIVE
+
+    // Exploit the gap: the FIRST time the acquire reads a missing lock, a live
+    // competitor acquires it before our write. An ATOMIC acquire (exclusive
+    // create) must then fail; a check-then-write clobbers and proceeds.
+    const realRead = io.fs.readFileSync.bind(io.fs);
+    const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
+    let injected = false;
+    io.fs.readFileSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ enc) => {
+      if (String(p) === LOCK && !injected) {
+        try {
+          return realRead(p, enc);
+        } catch (e) {
+          injected = true;
+          io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+          realWrite(LOCK, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'competitor' }));
+          throw e;
+        }
+      }
+      return realRead(p, enc);
+    };
+
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a lock acquired by a live competitor in the gap must refuse the run');
+    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned when the lock was lost in the gap');
+    assert.match(JSON.parse(io.files()[LOCK]).runId, /competitor/, "the competitor's lock is not clobbered");
+  });
+});
+
+// ===========================================================================
+// G4 (A4) — record in-flight child groups; kill recorded orphans on reclaim.
+describe('loop run — child-group recording + kill-on-reclaim (G4)', () => {
+  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
+
+  it('RED (G4): each spawn persists a child-start record {pid, pgid} under .handoff/loop/ (for recovery adoption)', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    let n = 0;
+    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ _spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
+      n += 1;
+      return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath, pid: 8000 + n, pgid: 9000 + n });
+    };
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0);
+    // Some persisted file under .handoff/loop/ carries the reported child pgid(s).
+    const recorded = Object.entries(io.files()).some(([k, v]) => k.startsWith(`${DIR}/`) && /"pgid"\s*:\s*900[12]/.test(String(v)));
+    assert.ok(recorded, 'a child-start record with the pgid is persisted under .handoff/loop/');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (G4): on dead-lock reclaim, a still-alive recorded child group is KILLED (io.processKill) before resuming', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    const killCalls = [];
+    io.processKill = (/** @type {number} */ pid, /** @type {any} */ sig) => { killCalls.push({ pid, sig }); return true; };
+    // The dead supervisor's orphan group (pgid 9001) is still alive; its lock is dead.
+    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ p) => p === 9001;
+    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 999999, startTime: 7, runId: 'crashed' }));
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/children.ndjson`, JSON.stringify({ childId: '001-plan', pid: 8001, pgid: 9001, startedAt: T0 }) + '\n');
+
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `the reclaiming run completes after reaping orphans; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.ok(killCalls.some((k) => Math.abs(k.pid) === 9001), `the orphan group 9001 was killed on reclaim; kills: ${JSON.stringify(killCalls)}`);
+  });
+});
+
+// ===========================================================================
+// G6 (B6) — `baton loop resume`: PARKED -> running; ESCALATED refused.
+describe('loop run — resume (G6)', () => {
+  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
+  const seedState = (over) => ({
+    schema: 'baton/loop-state@1',
+    runId: 'loop-r',
+    goal: 'Ship the loop',
+    phaseCount: 2,
+    phaseIndex: 0,
+    iterations: {},
+    status: 'running',
+    parkReason: null,
+    escalation: null,
+    smokeApproval: null,
+    createdAt: T0,
+    journalSeq: 0,
+    ...over,
+  });
+
+  it('RED (G6): `baton loop resume` transitions a PARKED run back to running and continues (spawns the pending phase)', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/state.json`, JSON.stringify(seedState({ status: 'parked', parkReason: 'operator paused' }), null, 2) + '\n');
+
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['resume'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `resume continues a parked run; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.ok(io.__runner.calls.length >= 1, 'resume spawned the pending phase');
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.DONE, 'the resumed run completes');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (G6): resume on an ESCALATED run is REFUSED (escalation stays operator-only, no spawn)', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/state.json`, JSON.stringify(seedState({ status: 'escalated', escalation: { gate: 'gate-1', iteration: 5 } }), null, 2) + '\n');
+
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['resume'], io);
+    assert.notEqual(code, 0, 'an escalated run cannot be resumed');
+    assert.match(io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText(), /escalat|operator/i, 'the refusal explains escalation is operator-only');
+    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'resume never spawns on an escalated run');
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, 'the run stays escalated');
+  });
+});

+    assert.match(io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText(), /escalat|operator/i, 'the refusal explains escalation is operator-only');
+    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'resume never spawns on an escalated run');
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, 'the run stays escalated');
+  });
+});

   483	  it('RED (B8): a competitor lock that appears in the check→write GAP is NOT clobbered — the run refuses (exit 1)', async () => {
   484	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
   485	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
   486	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   487	    io.processAlive = (p) => p === 55555; // the competitor supervisor is LIVE
   488	
   489	    // Exploit the gap: the FIRST time the acquire reads a missing lock, a live
   490	    // competitor acquires it before our write. An ATOMIC acquire (exclusive
   491	    // create) must then fail; a check-then-write clobbers and proceeds.
   492	    const realRead = io.fs.readFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   493	    const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   494	    let injected = false;
   495	    io.fs.readFileSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ enc) => {
   496	      if (String(p) === LOCK && !injected) {
   497	        try {
   498	          return realRead(p, enc);
   499	        } catch (e) {
   500	          injected = true;
   501	          io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
   502	          realWrite(LOCK, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'competitor' }));
   503	          throw e;
   504	        }
   505	      }
   506	      return realRead(p, enc);
   507	    };
   508	
   509	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
   510	    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a lock acquired by a live competitor in the gap must refuse the run');
   511	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned when the lock was lost in the gap');
   512	    assert.match(JSON.parse(io.files()[LOCK]).runId, /competitor/, "the competitor's lock is not clobbered");

    91	  const lockProbeRef = { fn: /** @type {null | (() => boolean)} */ (null) };
    92	  let mainSha = MAIN_SHA; // mutable so a child can "move main" mid-subtask
    93	
    94	  const list = () => {
    95	    const entries = [{ path: '/repo', branch: 'main' }];
    96	    for (const [path, branch] of added) entries.push({ path, branch: branchAt.get(path) ?? branch });
    97	    return entries.map((e) => `worktree ${e.path}\nHEAD ${MAIN_SHA}\nbranch refs/heads/${e.branch}\n`).join('\n');
    98	  };
    99	
   100	  const fn = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   101	    const argstr = args.join(' ');
   102	    calls.push({ cmd, args, argstr, cwd: opts?.cwd, lockPresent: lockProbeRef.fn ? lockProbeRef.fn() : undefined });
   103	    if (cmd !== 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   104	
   105	    // Explicit overrides win.
   138	  fn.git = () => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git');
   139	  fn.issued = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.some((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   140	  fn.matching = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr));
   141	  fn.withCwd = (/** @type {RegExp} */ re, /** @type {string} */ cwd) => calls.filter((c) => c.cmd === 'git' && re.test(c.argstr) && c.cwd === cwd);
   142	  fn.setLockProbe = (/** @type {() => boolean} */ f) => { lockProbeRef.fn = f; };
   143	  fn.moveMain = (/** @type {string} */ sha) => { mainSha = sha; }; // a child "moves main" mid-subtask
   144	  fn.setBranch = (/** @type {string} */ cwd, /** @type {string} */ branch) => { branchAt.set(cwd, branch); };
   145	  return fn;
   146	}
   667	  it('RED (G5): a listed-but-raw-deleted seat self-heals (prune + re-add), not a crash', async () => {
   668	    // The seat is listed by git but its dir is missing on the fs -> setup/preflight
   669	    // must self-heal (worktree prune + re-add) rather than crash git status.
   670	    const git = pipelineGit();
   671	    const io = makePipeRepo({ spec: pipelineSpec({ subtasks: [{ id: 't1', title: 'only' }] }), git, runner: undefined });
   672	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, cleanSubtask());
   673	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   674	    // Simulate a prior run that listed wt-a but whose dir was raw-deleted: seed
   675	    // git's worktree list to include wt-a while the fs has no such dir.
   676	    io.__git.setBranch(WT_A, 'baton/wt-a/base');
   677	    await assert.doesNotReject(async () => { await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io); }, 'a raw-deleted seat must self-heal, not crash');
   678	    assert.ok(io.__git.issued(/worktree prune/), 'the stale worktree metadata was pruned during self-heal');
   679	  });

    49	// or allows a Bash entry that is NOT a scoped read-only git command. A reviewer
    50	// may carry `Bash(git diff:*)` / `Bash(git log:*)` / `Bash(git show:*)` /

    57	  const bash = tools.split(',').map((t) => t.trim()).filter((t) => /^Bash\b/.test(t));
    58	  return bash.some((t) => !READONLY_GIT_BASH.test(t));

    30	  run-lock acquisition shared with loop run; stale branch → preflight park.
    31	
    32	- **G3 (A3 + B4) — Pipeline ignores child results/classification.**
    33	  Writer results are discarded: BLOCKED/limit/timeout/unparseable writers
    34	  proceed to review and can merge empty branches; pipeline never classifies
    35	  any child log and has no failover.
    36	  Fix: classify every child log (as loop run does), route limit classes to
    37	  runFailover, writer BLOCKED → the retry cap, refuse review of an empty
    38	  branch.
    39	
    40	- **G4 (A4) — Recovery never adopts in-flight children.**
    41	  superviseChild detaches process groups but nothing records them; a
    42	  supervisor death leaves live children, and lock reclaim neither kills nor
    43	  adopts them.
    44	  Fix: journal child start {pid, pgid} before spawn; on dead-lock reclaim
    45	  kill recorded groups (provably-dead discipline), then continue or park.
    46	
    47	## Major
    48	
    49	- **G5 (A5 + B5) — Worktree guard composition + reviewer blindness.**
    50	  selfHealWorktree is never called; reviewer seats spawn without preflight;
    51	  a listed-but-raw-deleted worktree crashes git status in a missing cwd;
    52	  claude read-only reviewers (Read,Grep,Glob) cannot run git diff, and the
    53	  subject branch is not checked out in the reviewing seat.
    54	  Fix: self-heal during setup/preflight; preflight reviewer seats; catch
    55	  git preflight errors → park; claude reviewer allowlist gains scoped
    56	  read-only git (diff/log/show); reviewer prompt reviews main..branch via
    57	  git (refs are shared across worktrees).
    58	
    59	- **G6 (B6) — Parked runs are terminal.** No CLI path emits RESUME while
    60	  the park/escalation messages instruct resuming.
    61	  Fix: `baton loop resume` (PARKED only; escalation stays operator-only);
    62	  correct the messages.
    63	
    64	- **G7 (B7) — Smoke gate keyed to the literal 'smoke-build' id.** A renamed
    65	  phase with smoke.cmd set silently skips the human gate.
    66	  Fix: validateLoopSpec rejects smoke.cmd without a smoke-build phase.
    67	
    68	## Minor (notes — recorded, selectively folded)
    69	
    70	- G8 (A6 + B9): --detach, probe integration, pipeline smoke gate, findings
    71	  persistence — explicitly recorded as deferred (plan + README note).
    72	- G9 (B10): constraint-1 clauses nominal (no reviews/ artifacts asserted;
    73	  journal events lack phase ids).
    74	- G10 (B11): maxChildrenPerPhase enforced globally, not per phase.
    75	- G11 (B12): codex 'tokens used' marker placement unverified against a live
    76	  transcript.
    77	- G12 (B13): unbounded in-memory child output buffering; classification
    78	  reads the capped log.
    79	- G13 (B14): child logs bypass redaction and the purge walk.
    80	- G14 (B15): hygiene — hardcoded 'main' trunk, to:'claude-code' resolver
    81	  hint, naive smoke.cmd split, stale RED comments in the e2e file.
    82	
    83	## Disposition plan
    84	
    85	Fold G1–G7 now (tests via the test-author, verifier re-entry, then
    86	implementation); record G8 deferrals in the plan; fold the cheap parts of
    87	G14 (stale comments); carry G9–G13 as documented v1.1 hardening items
    88	unless a reviewer re-raises them as gate-blocking. Re-review by both
    89	reviewers after the fold (iteration 2; cap 5 per reviewer).

codex
Ran the allowed four-suite command: 117 tests, 97 pass, 20 fail. `npm run typecheck` is green. Several reds are legitimate, but the pin set is not verifier-approved yet.

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [high] — The writer failover pin does not execute its intended assertion: it calls `ownedBundle(...)` but `pipeline-run.test.mjs` has no local definition/import, so the red is a `ReferenceError`, not evidence that writer limit classification is missing. The unresolved call is at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:623); the analogous helper exists only in [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:69). — Fix: add/import the bundle fixture in `pipeline-run.test.mjs`, then assert the first failed writer does not spawn a reviewer and the relaunch uses the failover model.

2. [high] — The cap-refund pin does not genuinely prove state accumulation across two invocations. The first invocation can run past the three scripted BLOCKED reviews because `fakeRunner` defaults exhausted queues to APPROVED at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:153), while the test only asserts the first run is “not escalated” at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:585). The second run then collides with fake branch state from `checkout -b` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:122), so it fails for stale-branch reasons, not cap refund. — Fix: leave a deliberate running/parked/crash-resumable state with exactly 3 persisted iterations, then resume and assert attempts 4/5 persist and attempt 6 escalates.

3. [high] — The raw-deleted worktree pin’s fixture does not actually make `git worktree list` include `wt-a`: `list()` emits only entries from `added` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:94), but the test calls only `setBranch`, which mutates `branchAt` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:144). The test comment claims the list includes `wt-a` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:674), but the fake does not model that. — Fix: add an explicit fake-git seed for listed worktrees, with missing cwd behavior that crashes unless `selfHealWorktree` prunes and re-adds.

4. [high] — The G4 child-start pin is too weak for the finding. The finding requires child-start `{pid, pgid}` journaling before recovery needs it at [findings.md](/Users/saksham/baton/reviews/2026-07-19-gate-2-milestone-b/findings.md:40), but the test accepts any file under `.handoff/loop/` containing a `pgid` after the fake runner has already returned at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:521) and [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:531). It does not require `children.ndjson`, `childId`, `startedAt`, or before-spawn/before-completion ordering. — Fix: assert the exact `children.ndjson` record shape and add an ordering seam that proves the record exists while the child is still in flight.

5. [high] — The dead-lock reclaim kill pin does not prove kill-before-resume ordering. It seeds `children.ndjson` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:545) and checks a kill happened by the end at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:549), but a wrong implementation could spawn/resume first and kill later. — Fix: make the fake runner assert the orphan group was killed before its first spawn, and record ordered events for `processKill` vs `superviseChild`.

6. [medium] — The B8 race pin is overfit to a read-based implementation. It injects the competitor only inside `io.fs.readFileSync(LOCK)` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:495), while a valid exclusive `mkdir`/`open wx` implementation could acquire without reading that path and would not trigger the test’s competitor. Current code does have the check/read then write shape at [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:142) and [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:162), so the red is meaningful against today’s code but not implementation-neutral. — Fix: move the race injection to the create/rename primitive or add an explicit exclusive-create companion that passes for `wx`/`mkdir` and fails for check-then-write.

7. [medium] — G5’s reviewer-blindness sub-item is not fully pinned. The finding requires the reviewer prompt to review `main..branch` via git at [findings.md](/Users/saksham/baton/reviews/2026-07-19-gate-2-milestone-b/findings.md:49), but the tests only identify reviewer calls by the prompt containing `subtask-reviewer` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:201) and assert model/cwd/read-only at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:323). — Fix: assert the reviewer prompt contains an explicit `git diff main..baton/wt-*/subtask-*` or equivalent `main..branch` instruction.

8. [low] — `claudeGrantsWrite` still fails open for write-capable Claude tools that are not literal `Write` or standalone `Edit`: the helper only checks acceptEdits, `Write`/`Edit`, and non-scoped Bash at [loop-children.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-children.test.mjs:53), and the no-write assertion reuses the same narrow `Write|Edit` test at [loop-children.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-children.test.mjs:113). — Fix: fail closed by allowlisting known read-only tools, or explicitly reject all known write/edit tool names including composite edit tools.
